### Crime

Main article: [Crime in Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_in_Detroit)

Further information: [Detroit Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Police_Department)



A 2025 Ford Police Interceptor



Detroit Police Underwater Recovery Team boat

Detroit has gained notoriety for its high amount of crime, having struggled with it for decades. The number of homicides in 1974 was 714.[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] The homicide rate in 2022 was the third highest in the nation at 50.0 per 100,000.[^cite_note-266] Downtown typically has lower crime than national and state averages.[^cite_note-MIC-267] According to a 2007 analysis, Detroit officials note about 65 to 70 percent of homicides in the city were drug related,[^cite_note-analysis-268] with the rate of unsolved murders roughly 70%.[^cite_note-time-256]

Although the rate of violent crime dropped 11% in 2008,[^cite_note-269] violent crime in Detroit has not declined as much as the national average from 2007 to 2011.[^cite_note-270] The violent crime rate is one of the highest in the United States. "Neighborhoodscout.com" reported a crime rate of 62.18 per 1,000 residents for property crimes, and 16.73 per 1,000 for violent crimes (compared to national figures of 32 per 1,000 for property crimes and 5 per 1,000 for violent crime in 2008).[^cite_note-Detroit_crime_rates_and_statistics-271] In 2012, crime in the city was among the reasons for more expensive car insurance.[^cite_note-car_insurance-272]

Areas of the city adjacent to the Detroit River are also patrolled by the [United States Border Patrol](567453.md).[^cite_note-273]

## Economy

See also: [Planning and development in Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planning_and_development_in_Detroit)

Several major corporations are based in the city, including three Fortune 500 companies. The most heavily represented sectors are manufacturing (particularly automotive), finance, technology, and health care. The most significant companies based in Detroit include [General Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Motors), [Rocket Mortgage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocket_Mortgage), [Ally Financial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ally_Financial), [Compuware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compuware), [Shinola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shinola_Detroit), [American Axle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Axle), [Little Caesars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Caesars), [DTE Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DTE_Energy), [Lowe Campbell Ewald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lowe_Campbell_Ewald), [Blue Cross Blue Shield of Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Cross_Blue_Shield_of_Michigan), and [Rossetti Architects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rossetti_Architects).

About 80,500 people work in downtown Detroit, comprising one-fifth of the city's employment base.[^cite_note-Marketprofile-274][^cite_note-275] Aside from the numerous Detroit-based companies listed above, downtown contains large offices for [Comerica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comerica), [Stellantis](64563392.md) (formerly Chrysler), [Fifth Third Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth_Third_Bank), [HP Enterprise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HP_Enterprise_Services), [Deloitte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deloitte), [PricewaterhouseCoopers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PricewaterhouseCoopers), [KPMG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KPMG), and [Ernst & Young](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernst_&_Young). [Ford Motor Company](30433662.md) is in the adjacent city of [Dearborn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dearborn,_Michigan).[^cite_note-276]



[Hudson's Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hudson's_Detroit), completed in 2025, is the city's [second-tallest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Detroit) skyscraper.



[Detroit Financial District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Financial_District)

Thousands more employees work in Midtown, north of the central business district. Midtown's anchors are the city's largest single employer [Detroit Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Medical_Center), [Wayne State University](302260.md), and the [Henry Ford Health System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Ford_Health_System) in New Center. Midtown is also home to watchmaker [Shinola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shinola_\(retail_company\)) and an array of small and startup companies. [New Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Center,_Detroit) bases TechTown, a research and business incubator hub that is part of the Wayne State University system.[^cite_note-277] Like downtown, [Corktown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corktown,_Detroit) Is experiencing growth with the new Ford Corktown Campus under development.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279]

Many downtown employers are relatively new, as there has been a marked trend of companies moving from satellite suburbs into the downtown core.[^cite_note-280] Compuware completed its [world headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Campus_Martius) in downtown in 2003. [OnStar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OnStar), [Blue Cross Blue Shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Cross_Blue_Shield_of_Michigan), and [HP Enterprise Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HP_Enterprise_Services) are at the Renaissance Center. [PricewaterhouseCoopers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PricewaterhouseCoopers) Plaza offices are adjacent to [Ford Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford_Field), and [Ernst & Young](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernst_&_Young) completed its office building at [One Kennedy Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_Kennedy_Square) in 2006. Perhaps most prominently, in 2010, Quicken Loans, one of the largest mortgage lenders, relocated its world headquarters and 4,000 employees to downtown Detroit, consolidating its suburban offices.[^cite_note-Howes-281] In July 2012, the U.S. Patent and Trademark Office opened its Elijah J. McCoy Satellite Office in the Rivertown/Warehouse District as its first location outside Washington, D.C.'s metropolitan area.[^cite_note-282]

In April 2014, the [United States Department of Labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Labor) reported the city's unemployment rate at 14.5%.[^cite_note-MILMI2-283] In 2023 the city of Detroit reported a poverty rate of 33.8%.[^cite_note-284]

The city of Detroit and other [public–private partnerships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public%E2%80%93private_partnership) have attempted to catalyze the region's growth by facilitating the building and historical rehabilitation of residential high-rises in the downtown, creating a zone that offers many business tax incentives, creating recreational spaces such as the Detroit RiverWalk, [Campus Martius Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Campus_Martius_Park), [Dequindre Cut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dequindre_Cut) Greenway, and Green Alleys in Midtown. The city has cleared sections of land while retaining some historically significant vacant buildings to spur redevelopment;[^cite_note-285] even though it has struggled with finances, the city issued bonds in 2008 to provide funding for ongoing work to demolish blighted properties.[^cite_note-NextDetroit-188] Two years earlier, downtown reported \$1.3 billion in restorations and new developments which increased the number of construction jobs in the city.[^cite_note-partnership-174] In the decade prior to 2006, downtown gained more than \$15 billion in new investment from private and public sectors.[^cite_note-286]

Despite the city's recent financial issues, many developers remain unfazed by Detroit's problems.[^cite_note-287] Midtown is one of the most successful areas within Detroit to have a residential occupancy rate of 96%.[^cite_note-288] Numerous developments have been recently completed or are in various stages of construction. These include the \$82 million reconstruction of downtown's [David Whitney Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Whitney_Building) (now an [Aloft Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aloft_Hotel) and luxury residences), the Woodward Garden Block Development in Midtown, the residential conversion of the [David Broderick Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Broderick_Tower) in downtown, the rehabilitation of the [Book Cadillac Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_Cadillac_Hotel) (now a Westin and luxury condos) and [Fort Shelby Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Shelby_Hotel) (now Doubletree) also in downtown, and various smaller projects.[^cite_note-289][^cite_note-partnership-174]

Downtown's population of young professionals is growing, and retail is expanding.[^cite_note-Detroitres-290][^cite_note-291] A study in 2007 found out that Downtown's new residents are predominantly young professionals (57% are ages 25 to 34, 45% have bachelor's degrees, and 34% have a master's or professional degree),[^cite_note-Marketprofile-274][^cite_note-Detroitres-290][^cite_note-Detroitstudy-292] a trend which has hastened over the last decade. Since 2006, \$9 billion has been invested in downtown and surrounding neighborhoods; \$5.2 billion of which has come in 2013 and 2014.[^cite_note-293] Construction activity, particularly rehabilitation of historic downtown buildings, has increased markedly. As of 2014, the number of vacant downtown buildings has dropped from nearly 50 to around 13.[^cite_note-Crain-294]

In 2013 [Meijer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meijer), a midwestern retail chain, opened its first supercenter store in Detroit;[^cite_note-295] this was a \$20 million, 190,000-square-foot store in the northern portion of the city and it also is the centerpiece of a new \$72 million shopping center named Gateway Marketplace.[^cite_note-296] In 2015 Meijer opened its second supercenter store in the city.[^cite_note-297] In 2019 [JPMorgan Chase](231001.md) announced plans to invest \$50 million more in affordable housing, job training, and entrepreneurship by the end of 2022, growing its investment to \$200 million.[^cite_note-298]

Near Zug Island, the southwest part of the city was developed over a 1,500-acre (610 ha) salt mine that is 1,100 feet (340 m) below the surface. The [Detroit salt mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_salt_mine) run by the Detroit Salt Company has over 100 miles (160 km) of roads within.[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300]

## Arts and culture

Main article: [Culture of Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Detroit)

In the central portions of Detroit, the population of young professionals, artists, and other transplants is growing and retail is expanding.[^cite_note-Detroitres-290] This dynamic is luring additional new residents, and former residents returning from other cities, to the city's Downtown along with the revitalized Midtown and New Center areas.[^cite_note-Marketprofile-274][^cite_note-Detroitres-290][^cite_note-Detroitstudy-292]

A desire to be closer to the urban scene has attracted some young professionals to reside in inner ring suburbs such as [Ferndale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferndale,_Michigan) and [Royal Oak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Oak,_Michigan).[^cite_note-301] The proximity to Windsor provides for views and nightlife, along with Ontario's minimum drinking age of 19.[^cite_note-Windsornightlife-302] A 2011 study by [Walk Score](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walk_Score) recognized Detroit for its above average walkability among large U.S. cities.[^cite_note-303] About two-thirds of suburban residents occasionally dine and attend cultural events or take in professional games in the city.[^cite_note-Visitor_poll-304]

### Music

Main article: [Music of Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Detroit)

Live music has been a prominent feature of Detroit's nightlife since the late 1940s, bringing the city recognition under the nickname "Motown".[^cite_note-305] The metropolitan area has many nationally prominent live music venues. Concerts hosted by [Live Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_Nation_Entertainment) perform throughout the Detroit area. The theater venue circuit is the United States' second largest and hosts [Broadway performances](725252.md).[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307]



2025 [Detroit International Jazz Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_International_Jazz_Festival)

The city has a rich musical heritage and has contributed to many genres over the decades.[^cite_note-Almanac2-308] Important music events include the [Detroit International Jazz Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_International_Jazz_Festival), the [Detroit Electronic Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Electronic_Music_Festival), the Motor City Music Conference (MC2), the Urban Organic Music Conference, the Concert of Colors, and the hip-hop Summer Jamz festival.[^cite_note-Almanac2-308]

In the 1940s, [Detroit blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_blues) artist [John Lee Hooker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Lee_Hooker) became a long-term resident in the [Delray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delray,_Detroit) neighborhood. Hooker, among other important blues musicians, migrated from his home in Mississippi, bringing the [Delta blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delta_blues) to Detroit. Hooker recorded for [Fortune Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_Records), the biggest pre-Motown blues/soul label. During the 1950s, the city became a center for jazz, with stars performing in the Black Bottom neighborhood.[^cite_note-Woodford-309] Prominent emerging jazz musicians included trumpeter [Donald Byrd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Byrd) (who attended Cass Tech and performed with [Art Blakey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Blakey) and [the Jazz Messengers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jazz_Messengers) early in his career) and saxophonist [Pepper Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pepper_Adams) (who enjoyed a solo career and accompanied Byrd on several albums). The Graystone International Jazz Museum documents jazz in Detroit.[^cite_note-310]

Other prominent Motor City R\&B stars in the 1950s and early 1960s were [Nolan Strong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nolan_Strong), [Andre Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andre_Williams_\(musician\)), and [Nathaniel Mayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nathaniel_Mayer)—who all scored local and national hits on the [Fortune Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_Records) label. According to [Smokey Robinson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smokey_Robinson), Strong was a primary influence on his voice as a teenager. The Fortune label, a family-operated label on Third Avenue, was owned by the husband-and-wife team of Jack Brown and Devora Brown. Fortune—which also released country, gospel and rockabilly LPs and 45s—laid the groundwork for Motown, which became Detroit's most legendary record label.[^cite_note-Carson-311]



Studio A control room in the [Hitsville U.S.A.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hitsville_U.S.A.) building on W. [Grand Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Boulevard_\(Detroit\)). This building was [Motown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motown)'s first headquarters and recording studio.

[Berry Gordy, Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berry_Gordy,_Jr.) founded [Motown Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motown_Records), which rose to prominence during the 1960s and early 1970s with acts such as [Stevie Wonder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stevie_Wonder), [the Temptations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Temptations), [the Four Tops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Four_Tops), [Smokey Robinson & the Miracles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smokey_Robinson_&_the_Miracles), [Diana Ross & the Supremes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diana_Ross_&_the_Supremes), [the Jackson 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jackson_5), [Martha and the Vandellas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martha_and_the_Vandellas), [the Spinners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Spinners_\(American_group\)), [Gladys Knight & the Pips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gladys_Knight_&_the_Pips), [the Marvelettes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Marvelettes), [the Elgins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Elgins), [the Monitors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Monitors_\(American_band\)), [the Velvelettes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Velvelettes), and [Marvin Gaye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marvin_Gaye). Artists were backed by in-house vocalists[^cite_note-312] [the Andantes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Andantes) and [the Funk Brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Funk_Brothers).

"The Motown sound" played an important role in the crossover appeal with popular music, since it was the first African American–owned record label to primarily feature African-American artists. Gordy moved Motown to Los Angeles in 1972 to pursue film production, but the company has since returned to Detroit. [Aretha Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aretha_Franklin), another Detroit R\&B star, carried the Motown sound; however, she did not record with Berry's Motown label.[^cite_note-Almanac2-308]

Local artists and bands rose to prominence in the 1960s and 70s, including the [MC5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MC5), [Glenn Frey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenn_Frey), [the Stooges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Stooges), [Bob Seger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Seger), [Amboy Dukes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Amboy_Dukes_\(band\)) featuring [Ted Nugent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Nugent), [Mitch Ryder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitch_Ryder) and The Detroit Wheels, [Rare Earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rare_Earth_\(band\)), [Alice Cooper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_Cooper), and [Suzi Quatro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suzi_Quatro). The group [Kiss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiss_\(band\)) emphasized the city's connection with rock in the song "[Detroit Rock City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Rock_City)" and the movie produced in 1999. In the 1980s, Detroit was an important center of the [hardcore punk rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hardcore_punk_rock) underground with many nationally known bands coming out of the city and its suburbs, such as [the Necros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Necros), [the Meatmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Meatmen), and [Negative Approach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negative_Approach).[^cite_note-Carson-311]

In the 1990s and 2000s, the city produced many influential [hip-hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip-hop) artists, including [Eminem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eminem), the hip-hop artist with the highest cumulative sales, his rap group [D12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D12), hip-hop rapper and producer [Royce da 5'9"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royce_da_5'9%22), hip-hop producer [Denaun Porter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denaun_Porter), hip-hop producer [J Dilla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J_Dilla), rapper and musician [Kid Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kid_Rock) and rappers [Big Sean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Sean) and [Danny Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danny_Brown_\(rapper\)). The band [Sponge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sponge_\(band\)) toured and produced music.[^cite_note-Almanac2-308][^cite_note-Carson-311] The city also has an active [garage rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garage_rock) scene that has generated national attention with acts such as [the White Stripes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_White_Stripes), [the Von Bondies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Von_Bondies), [the Detroit Cobras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Detroit_Cobras), [the Dirtbombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Dirtbombs), [Electric Six](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_Six), and [the Hard Lessons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hard_Lessons).[^cite_note-Almanac2-308] Detroit is cited as the birthplace of [techno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Techno) music in the early 1980s.[^cite_note-Plexifilm-313] The city also lends its name to an early and pioneering genre of [electronic dance music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_dance_music), "[Detroit techno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_techno)". Featuring science fiction imagery and robotic themes, its futuristic style was greatly influenced by the geography of Detroit's urban decline and its industrial past.[^cite_note-Woodford-309] Prominent Detroit techno artists include [Juan Atkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Atkins), [Derrick May](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derrick_May_\(musician\)), [Kevin Saunderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Saunderson), and [Jeff Mills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Mills). The [Detroit Electronic Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Electronic_Music_Festival), now known as Movement, occurs annually in late May on Memorial Day Weekend, and takes place in [Hart Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hart_Plaza).

### Performing arts

Main article: [Performing arts in Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Performing_arts_in_Detroit)

See also: [Theatre in Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theatre_in_Detroit)



[The Fillmore Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Fillmore_Detroit)

Major theaters in Detroit include the [Fox Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Theatre_\(Detroit\)) (5,174 seats), [Music Hall Center for the Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_Hall_Center_for_the_Performing_Arts) (1,770 seats), the [Gem Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gem_Theatre_\(Detroit\)) (451 seats), [Masonic Temple Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Masonic_Temple) (4,404 seats), the [Detroit Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Opera_House) (2,765 seats), the [Fisher Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fisher_Theatre) (2,089 seats), [The Fillmore Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Fillmore_Detroit) (2,200 seats), [Saint Andrew's Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Andrew's_Hall), the [Majestic Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majestic_Theater_\(Detroit,_Michigan\)), and [Orchestra Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orchestra_Hall_\(Detroit\)) (2,286 seats), which hosts the renowned [Detroit Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Symphony_Orchestra). The [Nederlander Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nederlander_Organization), the largest controller of Broadway productions in New York City, originated with the purchase of the Detroit Opera House in 1922 by the Nederlander family.[^cite_note-Almanac2-308]

[Motown Motion Picture Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motown_Motion_Picture_Studios) with 535,000 square feet (49,700 m<sup>2</sup>) produces movies in Detroit and the surrounding area based at the Pontiac Centerpoint Business Campus for a film industry expected to employ over 4,000 people in the metro area.[^cite_note-314]

### Tourist attractions



[Detroit Historical Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Historical_Museum)

Detroit is home to the world's first [destination marketing organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destination_marketing_organization), the Detroit Metro Convention and Visitor's Bureau, also known as Visit Detroit.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316] Founded in 1896, the organization now operates at [211 West Fort Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/211_West_Fort_Street) as Visit Detroit.[^cite_note-317]

Because of its unique culture, distinctive architecture, and revitalization and urban renewal efforts in the 21st century, Detroit has enjoyed increased prominence as a tourist destination in recent years. *[The New York Times](30680.md)* listed Detroit as the ninth-best destination in its list of *52 Places to Go in 2017*,[^cite_note-52_Places_to_Go_in_2017-318] while travel guide publisher *[Lonely Planet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lonely_Planet)* named Detroit the second-best city in the world to visit in 2018.[^cite_note-Top_10_cities_to_visit_in_2018-319] *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* named Detroit as one of the 50 World's Greatest Places of 2022 to explore.[^cite_note-320]



[Detroit Institute of Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Institute_of_Arts)

Many of the area's prominent museums are in the historic [cultural center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Cultural_Center) neighborhood around Wayne State University and the [College for Creative Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_for_Creative_Studies). These museums include the [Detroit Institute of Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Institute_of_Arts), the [Detroit Historical Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Historical_Museum), [Charles H. Wright Museum of African American History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_H._Wright_Museum_of_African_American_History), the [Detroit Science Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Science_Center), as well as the main branch of the [Detroit Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Public_Library). Other cultural highlights include [Motown Historical Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hitsville_U.S.A.), the [Ford Piquette Avenue Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford_Piquette_Avenue_Plant) museum, the [Pewabic Pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pewabic_Pottery) studio and school, the [Tuskegee Airmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuskegee_Airmen) Museum, [Fort Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Wayne_\(Detroit\)), the Dossin Great Lakes Museum, the [Museum of Contemporary Art Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Contemporary_Art_Detroit), the [Contemporary Art Institute of Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_Art_Institute_of_Detroit), and the [Belle Isle Conservatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belle_Isle_Conservatory).

In 2010, the G.R. N'Namdi Gallery opened in a 16,000-square-foot (1,500 m<sup>2</sup>) complex in Midtown. Important history of America and the Detroit area are exhibited at [The Henry Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Henry_Ford) in [Dearborn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dearborn,_Michigan), the United States' largest indoor-outdoor museum complex.[^cite_note-321] The Detroit Historical Society provides information about tours of area churches, skyscrapers, and mansions. Inside Detroit hosts tours, educational programming, and a downtown welcome center. Other sites of interest are the [Detroit Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Zoo) in [Royal Oak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Oak,_Michigan), the [Cranbrook Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cranbrook_Art_Museum) in [Bloomfield Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomfield_Hills,_Michigan), the [Anna Scripps Whitcomb Conservatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Scripps_Whitcomb_Conservatory) on Belle Isle, and Walter P. Chrysler Museum in [Auburn Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auburn_Hills,_Michigan).[^cite_note-AIADetroit-170]



[Eastern Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Market,_Detroit)

[Greektown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greektown,_Detroit) and three downtown casino resort hotels serve as part of an entertainment hub. The [Eastern Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Market,_Detroit) farmer's distribution center is the largest open-air flowerbed market in the United States and has more than 150 foods and specialty businesses.[^cite_note-322] On Saturdays, about 45,000 people shop there.[^cite_note-Easternmarket1-323] The annual Detroit Festival of the Arts in Midtown draws about 350,000 people.[^cite_note-Midtown2-324]



The [Ford Piquette Avenue Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford_Piquette_Avenue_Plant), birthplace of the [Ford Model T](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford_Model_T) and the world's oldest car factory building open to the public

Annual summer events include the [Electronic Music Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Electronic_Music_Festival), [International Jazz Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_International_Jazz_Festival), the [Woodward Dream Cruise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodward_Dream_Cruise), the African World Festival, the country music Hoedown, Noel Night, and [Dally in the Alley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dally_in_the_Alley). Within downtown, Campus Martius Park hosts large events, including the annual Motown Winter Blast. As the world's traditional automotive center, the city hosts the [North American International Auto Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_International_Auto_Show). Held since 1924, America's Thanksgiving Parade is one of the nation's largest.[^cite_note-325] River Days, a five-day summer festival on the International Riverfront lead up to the [Windsor–Detroit International Freedom Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windsor%E2%80%93Detroit_International_Freedom_Festival) fireworks, which draw super sized-crowds ranging from hundreds of thousands to over three million people.[^cite_note-Visitor_poll-304][^cite_note-Almanac2-308][^cite_note-Winterblast-326]

## Sports

Main article: [Sports in Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Detroit)

See also: [U.S. cities with teams from four major sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._cities_with_teams_from_four_major_sports)



[Little Caesars Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Caesars_Arena), home of the [Detroit Red Wings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Red_Wings) and the [Detroit Pistons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Pistons)



[Comerica Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comerica_Park), home of the [American League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_League) [Detroit Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Tigers)



[Ford Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford_Field), home of the [Detroit Lions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Lions)

Detroit is one of four U.S. cities that have venues within the city representing the four major sports in North America. Detroit is the only city to have its four major sports teams play within its downtown district.[^cite_note-327] Detroit is also the only city that has a team in all "Big Four" leagues, but lacks an MLS team. Venues include: [Comerica Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comerica_Park) (home of [Major League Baseball](38776.md)'s [Detroit Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Tigers)), [Ford Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ford_Field) (home of the [National Football League](21211.md)'s [Detroit Lions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Lions)), and [Little Caesars Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Caesars_Arena) (home of the [National Hockey League](21809.md)'s [Detroit Red Wings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Red_Wings) and the [National Basketball Association](22093.md)'s [Detroit Pistons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Pistons)).

Detroit has won titles in all four of the major professional sports leagues. The Tigers have won four World Series titles (1935, 1945, 1968, and 1984). The Red Wings have won 11 [Stanley Cups](66968.md) ([1935–36](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1936_Stanley_Cup_Final), [1936–37](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1937_Stanley_Cup_Final), [1942–43](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1943_Stanley_Cup_Final), [1949–50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1950_Stanley_Cup_Final), [1951–52](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1952_Stanley_Cup_Final), [1953–54](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_Stanley_Cup_Final), [1954–55](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1955_Stanley_Cup_Final), [1996–97](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Stanley_Cup_Final), [1997–98](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Stanley_Cup_Final), [2001–02](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Stanley_Cup_Final), [2007–08](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Stanley_Cup_Final)) (the most by an American NHL franchise and also having the third most championships by an NHL team behind the [Toronto Maple Leafs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto_Maple_Leafs) and [Montreal Canadiens](42966.md) respectively).[^cite_note-328] The Lions have won 4 NFL titles ([1935](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1935_NFL_Championship_Game), [1952](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1952_NFL_Championship_Game), [1953](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1953_NFL_Championship_Game), [1957](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1957_NFL_Championship_Game)). The Pistons have won three NBA titles (1989, 1990, 2004).[^cite_note-Almanac2-308] In the years following the mid-1930s, Detroit was referred to as the "City of Champions" after the Tigers, Lions, and Red Wings captured the three major professional sports championships in existence at the time in a seven-month period (the Tigers won the World Series in October 1935; the Lions won the NFL championship in December 1935; the Red Wings won the Stanley Cup in April 1936).[^cite_note-City_of_Champions-28]

Founded in 2012 as a semi-professional soccer club, [Detroit City FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_City_FC) now plays professional soccer in the [USL Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USL_Championship). Nicknamed, *Le Rouge*, the club are two-time champions of [NISA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Independent_Soccer_Association) since joining in 2020. They play their home matches in [Keyworth Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keyworth_Stadium), which is located in the enclave of [Hamtramck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamtramck,_Michigan).[^cite_note-329]

In college sports, Detroit's central location within the [Mid-American Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-American_Conference) (MAC) has made it a frequent site for the league's championship events. While the MAC Basketball Tournament moved permanently to [Cleveland](5951.md) starting in 2000, the MAC Football Championship Game has been played at Ford Field since 2004 and annually attracts 25,000 to 30,000 fans. The University of Detroit Mercy has an [NCAA Division I](669931.md) program, and Wayne State University has both NCAA Division I and [II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_II) programs. The NCAA football [GameAbove Sports Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GameAbove_Sports_Bowl) (formerly, Quick Lane Bowl) is held at Ford Field each December.

The city hosted the 2005 [MLB All-Star Game](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLB_All-Star_Game), [Super Bowl XL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XL) in 2006, the 2006 and [2012 World Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_World_Series), [WrestleMania 23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WrestleMania_23) in 2007, and the [NCAA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA) [Final Four](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Final_Four_\(college_basketball\)) in April 2009. The [Detroit Indy Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Indy_Grand_Prix) is held in Belle Isle Park. In 2007, open-wheel racing returned to Belle Isle with both [Indy Racing League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy_Racing_League) and [American Le Mans Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Le_Mans_Series) Racing.[^cite_note-2007IndyRacing-330] From 1982 to 1988, Detroit held the [Detroit Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Grand_Prix_\(Formula_One\)), at the [Detroit street circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_street_circuit).

Several notable athletes came from Detroit. In 1932, [Eddie "The Midnight Express" Tolan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eddie_Tolan) from Detroit won the 100- and 200-meter races and two gold medals at the [1932 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1932_Summer_Olympics). He was the first black athlete to win two Olympic gold medals.[^cite_note-331] [Joe Louis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Louis) won the heavyweight championship of the world in 1937.[^cite_note-332]

Detroit has made the most [bids to host the Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_bids_for_the_Summer_Olympics) without ever being awarded the right to host the games, with seven unsuccessful bids submitted. Detroit bid for the [1944](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1944_Summer_Olympics), [1952](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1952_Summer_Olympics), [1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1956_Summer_Olympics), [1960](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1960_Summer_Olympics), [1964](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_Summer_Olympics), [1968](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Summer_Olympics), and [1972 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_Summer_Olympics), losing to [London](17867.md), [Helsinki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsinki), [Melbourne](17306237.md), [Rome](25458.md), [Tokyo](30057.md), [Mexico City](18987.md) and [Munich](19058.md) respectively.[^cite_note-Almanac2-308]

In 2024, Detroit hosted the [NFL draft](710502.md). Over 775,000 people were present in downtown Detroit over the course of the three-day event, making it the highest attended draft on record.[^cite_note-333]

## Government

Main article: [Government of Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Detroit)



The [Coleman A. Young Municipal Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coleman_A._Young_Municipal_Center) houses the headquarters of the government of the City of Detroit, as well as offices of the Wayne County government.

The city is governed pursuant to the [home rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home_rule) *Charter of the City of Detroit*. The government is run by a mayor, the nine-member [Detroit City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_City_Council), the eleven-member [Board of Police Commissioners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Board_of_Police_Commissioners), and a clerk. All of these officers are elected on a nonpartisan ballot, with the exception of four of the police commissioners, who are appointed by the mayor. Detroit has a "[strong mayoral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor%E2%80%93council_government)" system, with the mayor approving departmental appointments. The council approves budgets, but the mayor is not obligated to adhere to any earmarking. The city clerk supervises elections and is formally charged with the maintenance of municipal records. City ordinances and substantially large contracts must be approved by the council.[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-crcmich.org-335] The *Detroit City Code* is the [codification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Codification_\(law\)) of Detroit's [local ordinances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Local_ordinance).

Presently three Community Advisory Councils advise City Council representatives. Residents of each of Detroit's seven districts have the option of electing Community Advisory Councils.[^cite_note-336] The city clerk supervises elections and is formally charged with the maintenance of municipal records. Municipal elections for mayor, city council and city clerk are held at four-year intervals, in the year after presidential elections.[^cite_note-crcmich.org-335] Following a November 2009 referendum, seven council members will be elected from districts beginning in 2013 while two will continue to be elected at-large.[^cite_note-cdet-337]

Detroit's courts are state-administered and elections are nonpartisan. The Probate Court for Wayne County is in the [Coleman A. Young Municipal Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coleman_A._Young_Municipal_Center) in downtown. The Circuit Court is across Gratiot Avenue in the Frank Murphy Hall of Justice. The city is home to the Thirty-Sixth District Court, as well as the First District of the [Michigan Court of Appeals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan_Court_of_Appeals) and the [United States District Court for the Eastern District of Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_District_Court_for_the_Eastern_District_of_Michigan). The city provides law enforcement through the [Detroit Police Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Police_Department) and emergency services through the [Detroit Fire Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Fire_Department).[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339]

### Politics

See also: [List of mayors of Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mayors_of_Detroit)



[Mary Sheffield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Sheffield), the incumbent mayor of Detroit

Beginning with its incorporation in 1802, Detroit has had a total of [74 mayors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_mayors_of_Detroit). Detroit's last mayor from the [Republican Party](32070.md) was [Louis Miriani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Miriani), who served from 1957 to 1962. In 1973, the city elected its first black mayor, [Coleman Young](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coleman_Young). Despite development efforts, his combative style during his five terms in office was not well received by many suburban residents.[^cite_note-340] Mayor [Dennis Archer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Archer), a former Michigan Supreme Court Justice, refocused the city's attention on redevelopment with a plan to permit three casinos downtown. By 2008, three major casino resort hotels established operations in the city.[^cite_note-341]

In 2000, the city requested an investigation by the [United States Justice Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Justice_Department) into the Detroit Police Department which was concluded in 2003 over allegations regarding its use of force and civil rights violations. The city proceeded with a major reorganization of the Detroit Police Department.[^cite_note-342] In 2013, felony bribery charges were brought against seven building inspectors.[^cite_note-343] In 2016, further corruption charges were brought against 12 principals, a former school superintendent and supply vendor[^cite_note-344] for a \$12 million (\~\$15.3 million in 2024) kickback scheme.[^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346] However, law professor Peter Henning argues Detroit's corruption is not unusual for a city its size, especially when compared with Chicago.[^cite_note-347] In 2025, the city elected its first woman as mayor, [Mary Sheffield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Sheffield).[^cite_note-348]

Detroit is sometimes referred to as a [sanctuary city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanctuary_city) because it has "anti-profiling ordinances that generally prohibit local police from asking about the immigration status of people who are not suspected of any crime".[^cite_note-349] The city in recent years has been a stronghold for the [Democratic Party](5043544.md), with around 90% of votes in the city going to incumbent vice president, [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris), the Democratic candidate in the [2024 Presidential election](59568546.md).[^cite_note-350]

## Education

### Primary and secondary schools

See also: [Detroit Public Schools Community District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Public_Schools_Community_District) and [List of Detroit Public Schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Detroit_Public_Schools)

As of 2016 many K-12 students in Detroit frequently change schools, with some children having been enrolled in seven schools before finishing their K-12 careers. There is a concentration of senior high schools and [charter schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_schools) in the downtown area, which had wealthier residents and more gentrification relative to other parts of Detroit: Downtown, northwest Detroit, and northeast Detroit have 1,894, 3,742, and 6,018 students of high school age, respectively, while they have 11, three, and two high schools, respectively.[^cite_note-Zernike-351] As of 2016 because of the lack of public transportation and the lack of school bus services, many Detroit families have to rely on themselves to transport children to school.[^cite_note-Zernike-351]

With about 66,000 public school students (2011–12), the [Detroit Public Schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Public_Schools) (DPS) district is the largest [school district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_district) in Michigan. Detroit has an additional 56,000 charter school students for a combined enrollment of about 122,000 students.[^cite_note-charter-352][^cite_note-Enrollment-353] As of 2009 there are about as many students in charter schools as there are in district schools.[^cite_note-354] As of 2016 DPS continues to have the majority of the special education pupils. In addition, some Detroit students, as of 2016, attend public schools in other municipalities.[^cite_note-Zernike-351]

With growing charter schools enrollment as well as a continued exodus of population, the city planned to close many public schools.[^cite_note-charter-352] State officials report a 68% graduation rate for Detroit's public schools adjusted for those who change schools.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-Detroitk12-356] Traditional public and charter school students in the city have performed poorly on standardized tests. c. 2009 and 2011, while Detroit traditional public schools scored a record low on national tests, the publicly funded charter schools did even worse than the traditional public schools.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358] As of 2016 there were 30,000 excess openings in Detroit traditional public and charter schools, bearing in mind the number of K-12-aged children in the city. In 2016, [Kate Zernike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kate_Zernike) of *The New York Times* stated school performance did not improve despite the proliferation of charters, describing the situation as "lots of choice, with no good choice".[^cite_note-Zernike-351]

Detroit public schools students scored the lowest on tests of reading and writing of all major cities in the United States in 2015. Among eighth-graders, only 27% showed basic proficiency in math and 44% in reading.[^cite_note-359] Nearly half of Detroit's adults are [functionally illiterate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Functional_illiteracy).[^cite_note-360]

Detroit is served by various private schools, as well as parochial Roman Catholic schools operated by the [Archdiocese of Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Detroit). As of 2013 there are four Catholic grade schools and three Catholic high schools in the City of Detroit, with all of them in the city's west side.[^cite_note-Cathschool-361] The Archdiocese of Detroit lists a number of primary and secondary schools in the metro area as Catholic education has emigrated to the suburbs.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] Of the three Catholic high schools, two are operated by the Society of Jesus and the third is co-sponsored by the [Sisters, Servants of the Immaculate Heart of Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sisters,_Servants_of_the_Immaculate_Heart_of_Mary) and the [Congregation of St. Basil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congregation_of_St._Basil).[^cite_note-AOD-364][^cite_note-365]

### Post-secondary education

Detroit is home to several institutions of higher learning, including [Wayne State University](302260.md) and the [University of Detroit Mercy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Detroit_Mercy). [Grand Valley State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Valley_State_University)'s Detroit Center hosts workshops, seminars, professional development, and other large gatherings. [Sacred Heart Major Seminary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred_Heart_Major_Seminary), founded in 1919, is affiliated with [Pontifical University of Saint Thomas Aquinas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontifical_University_of_Saint_Thomas_Aquinas), *Angelicum* in Rome and offers pontifical degrees as well as civil undergraduate and graduate degrees. Other institutions in the city include the [College for Creative Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_for_Creative_Studies) and [Wayne County Community College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayne_County_Community_College). In June 2009, the [Michigan State University College of Osteopathic Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan_State_University_College_of_Osteopathic_Medicine) which is based in [East Lansing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Lansing,_Michigan) opened a satellite campus at the [Detroit Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Medical_Center).

Detroit has many [vocational training institutes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocational_education_in_the_United_States) that provide technical training to prepare students for careers in the skilled trades.

## Media

Main article: [Media in Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Detroit)

The *[Detroit Free Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Free_Press)* and *[The Detroit News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Detroit_News)* are the major daily newspapers in the city. From 1989 until their agreement expired in December 2025, both [broadsheet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadsheet) publications were managed by a [joint venture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_operating_agreement) called the [Detroit Media Partnership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Media_Partnership), with the *Free Press* under the ownership of [USA Today Co.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Today_Co.) (formerly Gannett) and the *News* under [MediaNews Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MediaNews_Group).[^cite_note-366] In March 2009, both newspapers reduced home delivery to three days per week, printed reduced newsstand issues on non-delivery days, and began focusing resources on Internet-based news delivery.[^cite_note-367] In January 2026, *USA Today* acquired the *News* with plans to continue operating each newspaper separately.[^cite_note-368]

The *[Metro Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro_Times)*, founded in 1980, is a weekly publication covering news, arts, and entertainment.[^cite_note-369] Founded in 1935 and based in Detroit, the *[Michigan Chronicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan_Chronicle)* is one of the oldest and most respected African-American weekly newspapers in America, covering politics, entertainment, sports, and community events.[^cite_note-370] Media philanthropy includes the *Detroit Free Press* high school journalism program, the Old Newsboys' Goodfellow Fund of Detroit, and the Community Foundation for Southeast Michigan's Detroit Journalism Engagement Fund.[^cite_note-371][^cite_note-372]

Detroit's television market is the 11th largest in the United States,[^cite_note-Nielson-373] according to estimates that do not include audiences in large areas of Ontario (including [Windsor](33596.md) and [Ottawa](22219.md)) who receive and watch Detroit television stations.[^cite_note-Nielson-373]

Detroit also has the 11th largest [radio market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_market) in the United States,[^cite_note-Arbitron-374] though this ranking also does not take into account Canadian audiences.[^cite_note-Arbitron-374] Nearby Canadian stations such as Windsor's [CKLW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CKLW) (whose jingles formerly proclaimed "CKLW - the Motor City") are popular in Detroit.[^cite_note-375]

## Infrastructure

### Health systems



[Children's Hospital of Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children's_Hospital_of_Michigan)



[Henry Ford Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Ford_Hospital)

There are over a dozen major hospitals, which include the [Detroit Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Medical_Center) (DMC), [Henry Ford Health System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Ford_Hospital), [St. John Health System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._John_Health), and the John D. Dingell VA Medical Center. DMC, a regional [Level I trauma center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Level_I_trauma_center), consists of Detroit Receiving Hospital and University Health Center, [Children's Hospital of Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children's_Hospital_of_Michigan), [Harper University Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harper_University_Hospital), [Hutzel Women's Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hutzel_Women's_Hospital), Kresge Eye Institute, [Rehabilitation Institute of Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rehabilitation_Institute_of_Michigan), [Sinai-Grace Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai-Grace_Hospital), and the [Karmanos Cancer Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karmanos_Cancer_Institute). DMC has more than 2,000 licensed beds and 3,000 affiliated physicians. It is the largest private employer in the city.[^cite_note-WSUMED-376] The center is staffed by physicians from the [Wayne State University School of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayne_State_University_School_of_Medicine), the largest single-campus medical school in the United States and the fourth largest medical school overall.[^cite_note-WSUMED-376]

DMC formally became a part of [Vanguard Health Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanguard_Health_Systems) on December 30, 2010, as a for-profit corporation. Vanguard has agreed to invest nearly \$1.5 B in the DMC complex.[^cite_note-Lane-377][^cite_note-Anstett-378] Vanguard has agreed to assume all debts and pension obligations.[^cite_note-Lane-377]

In 2011, DMC and Henry Ford Health System substantially increased investments in medical research facilities and hospitals in the city's Midtown and New Center.[^cite_note-Lane-377][^cite_note-Greene-379] In 2012, two major construction projects were begun in New Center. The Henry Ford Health System started the first phase of a \$500 million, 300-acre revitalization project, with the construction of a new \$30 million, 275,000-square-foot, *Medical Distribution Center* for [Cardinal Health, Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_Health,_Inc.)[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] and Wayne State University started construction on a new \$93 million, 207,000-square-foot, Integrative Biosciences Center (IBio).[^cite_note-Henderson-382][^cite_note-383] As many as 500 researchers and staff will work out of the IBio Center.[^cite_note-384]

### Transportation

With its proximity to Canada and its facilities, ports, major highways, rail connections and international airports, Detroit is an important transportation hub.&#x20;

#### Border crossings



[Ambassador Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambassador_Bridge)

Detroit has four border crossings: the [Gordie Howe International Bridge](26342191.md) which opened in 2026, the [Ambassador Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambassador_Bridge)[^cite_note-385] and the [Detroit–Windsor tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit%E2%80%93Windsor_tunnel) provide motor vehicle thoroughfares, with the [Michigan Central Railway Tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan_Central_Railway_Tunnel) providing railroad access to and from Canada. Until 2023, the [Detroit–Windsor Truck Ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit%E2%80%93Windsor_Truck_Ferry) serviced the border crossing near the Windsor Salt Mine and [Zug Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zug_Island).

In 2015 Canadian Transport Minister Lisa Raitt announced Canada agreed to pay the entire cost to build a \$250 million U.S. Customs plaza adjacent to the planned new Detroit–Windsor bridge, now the [Gordie Howe International Bridge](26342191.md). Canada had already planned to pay for 95% of the bridge, which will cost \$2.1 billion and is expected to open in early 2026.[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] "This allows Canada and Michigan to move the project forward immediately to its next steps which include further design work and property acquisition on the U.S. side of the border", Raitt said issued after she spoke in the House of Commons. [^cite_note-388]

#### Transit systems

See caption

A [QLINE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QLINE) [streetcar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Streetcar) arriving at the Congress Street station

Mass transit in the region is provided by bus services. The [Detroit Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Department_of_Transportation) provides service within city limits up to the outer edges of the city. From there, the [Suburban Mobility Authority for Regional Transportation (SMART)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suburban_Mobility_Authority_for_Regional_Transportation) provides service to the suburbs and the city regionally with local routes and SMART's FAST service. FAST is a new service provided by SMART which offers limited stops along major corridors throughout the Detroit metropolitan area connecting the suburbs to downtown. The new high-frequency service travels along three of Detroit's busiest corridors, Gratiot, Woodward, and Michigan, and only stops at designated FAST stops. Cross border service between the downtown areas of Windsor and Detroit is provided by [Transit Windsor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transit_Windsor) via the Tunnel Bus.[^cite_note-Tunnelbus-389] Intercity bus service is offered at the [Detroit Bus Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Bus_Station). [Greyhound Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greyhound_Lines), [Flixbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flixbus), [Indian Trails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Trails), and [Barons Bus Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barons_Bus_Lines) connect Detroit with numerous cities across the [Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwest).&#x20;



The [Greektown station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greektown_station) on the Detroit People Mover (DPM) [elevated railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elevated_railway)

An elevated rail system known as the [People Mover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_People_Mover), completed in 1987, provides daily service around a 2.94-mile (4.73 km) loop downtown. The [QLine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/QLine) serves as a link between the People Mover and the [Amtrak station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_station) via Woodward Avenue.[^cite_note-390]

The [Regional Transit Authority (RTA)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Transit_Authority_of_Southeast_Michigan) was established by an act of the Michigan legislature in 2012 to oversee and coordinate all existing regional mass transit operations, and to develop new transit services in the region. The RTA's first project was the introduction of RelfeX, a [limited-stop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limited-stop), cross-county bus service connecting downtown and midtown Detroit with Oakland county via Woodward avenue.[^cite_note-391]

#### Passenger and freight rail

[Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) provides service to Detroit, operating its *[Wolverine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolverine_\(train\))* service between Chicago and [Pontiac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontiac,_Michigan). The Amtrak station is in New Center north of downtown.

Freight railroad operations in the city of Detroit are provided by [Canadian National Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_National_Railway), [Canadian Pacific Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Pacific_Railway), [Conrail Shared Assets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conrail_Shared_Assets), [CSX Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSX_Transportation) and [Norfolk Southern Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_Southern_Railway), each of which have local yards within the city. Detroit is also served by the [Delray Connecting Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delray_Connecting_Railroad) and [Detroit Connecting Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Connecting_Railroad) shortlines.[^cite_note-392]

#### Airports

[Detroit Metropolitan Wayne County Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Metropolitan_Wayne_County_Airport) (DTW), the principal airport serving Detroit, is in nearby [Romulus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romulus,_Michigan). DTW is a primary hub for [Delta Air Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delta_Air_Lines) (following its acquisition of Northwest Airlines). The airport is connected to [Downtown Detroit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Detroit) by the [Suburban Mobility Authority for Regional Transportation (SMART)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suburban_Mobility_Authority_for_Regional_Transportation) FAST Michigan route.[^cite_note-393]

[Coleman A. Young International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coleman_A._Young_International_Airport) (DET), previously called Detroit City Airport, is on Detroit's northeast side; the airport now maintains only charter service and [general aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_aviation).[^cite_note-394] [Willow Run Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willow_Run_Airport), in western Wayne County near Ypsilanti, is a general aviation and cargo airport.[^cite_note-395]

#### Roads and freeways



[I-94](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I-94) runs east–west through Detroit. Pictured is the [Detroit Sign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detroit_Sign), located along eastbound I-94 on the city's southwest side.

Three road systems cross the city: the original French template, with avenues radiating from the waterfront, and true [north–south roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roads_and_freeways_in_metropolitan_Detroit) based on the [Northwest Ordinance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Ordinance) township system. The city is north of Windsor, Ontario. Detroit is the only major city along the Canada–U.S. border in which one travels south to cross into Canada.[^cite_note-396]

Metro Detroit has an extensive toll-free network of freeways administered by the [Michigan Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan_Department_of_Transportation). Four major Interstate Highways surround the city. Detroit is connected via [I-75](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_75_in_Michigan) and [I-96](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_96) to [Kings Highway 401](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ontario_Highway_401) and to major [Southern Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Ontario) cities such as [London, Ontario](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London,_Ontario) and the [Greater Toronto Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Toronto_Area). I-75 (Chrysler and Fisher freeways) is the region's main north–south route, serving [Flint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flint,_Michigan), [Pontiac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pontiac,_Michigan), [Troy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy,_Michigan), and Detroit, before continuing south (as the Detroit–Toledo and Seaway Freeways) to serve many of the communities along the shore of Lake Erie.[^cite_note-Cantor-397]

[I-94](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_94_in_Michigan) (Edsel Ford Freeway) runs east–west through Detroit and serves Ann Arbor to the west (where it continues to Chicago) and Port Huron to the northeast. The stretch of the I-94 freeway from Ypsilanti to Detroit was one of America's earlier limited-access highways. [Henry Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Ford) built it to link the factories at Willow Run and Dearborn during World War II. A portion was known as the Willow Run Expressway. The I-96 freeway runs northwest–southeast through Livingston, Oakland and Wayne counties and (as the Jeffries Freeway through Wayne County) has its eastern terminus in downtown Detroit.[^cite_note-Cantor-397]

[I-275](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_275_\(Michigan\)) runs north–south from I-75 in the south to the junction of I-96 and [I-696](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_696) in the north, providing a bypass through the western suburbs of Detroit. [I-375](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_375_\(Michigan\)) is a short spur route in downtown Detroit, an extension of the Chrysler Freeway. I-696 (Reuther Freeway) runs east–west from the junction of I-96 and I-275, providing a route through the northern suburbs of Detroit. Taken together, I-275 and I-696 form a semicircle around Detroit. Michigan state highways designated with the letter M serve to connect major freeways.[^cite_note-Cantor-397]

### Postal service

#### Floating post office



*[J.W. Westcott II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J.W._Westcott_II)* on the Detroit River

Detroit has a floating post office, the *[J. W. Westcott II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._W._Westcott_II)*, which serves lake freighters along the Detroit River. Its [ZIP Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ZIP_Code) is 48222.[^cite_note-398] The ZIP Code is used exclusively for the *J. W. Westcott II*, which makes it the only floating ZIP Code in the United States. It has a land-based office at 12 24th Street, just south of the Ambassador Bridge. The J.W. Westcott Company was established in 1874 by Captain John Ward Westcott as a maritime reporting agency to inform other vessels about port conditions,[^cite_note-399] and the *J. W. Westcott II* vessel began service in 1949 and is still in operation today.[^cite_note-400]

## Sister cities

Detroit's [sister cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sister_city) include the following:[^cite_note-401]

- [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) [Dubai](211583.md), United Arab Emirates
